President Michel Temer, revoked his status as a permanent resident in December and an arrest warrant was issued; Battisti then entered Bolivia illegally, and was arrested and extradited from Bolivia in January 2019. [\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-150)

### Operation Zelotes

In 2015 Lula, along with his former chief of staff [Gilberto Carvalho](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gilberto_Carvalho&action=edit&redlink=1) and five others, was indicted in a corruption probe as part of [Operation Zelotes,](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Operation_Zelotes&action=edit&redlink=1) regarding payment of R\$6 million in bribes (US \$1.5 million). Prosecutors alleged they helped pass Provisional Measure 471 (which was later converted into Law 12,218/2010) in 2009 in order to benefit the automotive companies [Grupo Caoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grupo_Caoa) and MMC[.\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-151) On 21 June 2021, Judge Frederico Botelho de Barros Viana of the 10th Federal Court of Brasilia acquitted all the accused, saying that the prosecution did not convincingly demonstrate that the defendants were involved in a criminal conspiracy.[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-152)

### Operation Car Wash: corruption investigation and prosecution

In 2014, Brazil began Operação Lava Jato (English: [Operation Car Wash\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Car_Wash), resulting in several arrests and convictions, including nine suits against Lula.

In April 2015, the [Public Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Prosecutor%27s_Office_(Brazil)) of Brazil opened an investigation into allegations of infl[uence peddling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Influence_peddling) by Lula, which alleged that between 2011 and 2014 he had lobbied for government contracts in foreign countries for the [Odebrecht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odebrecht) company and had also persuaded the Brazilian Development Bank to finance the projects in [Ghana,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghana) [Angola,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angola) [Cuba,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuba) and the [Dominican Republic.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominican_Republic)[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-153) In June 2015, [Marcelo Odebrecht,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo_Odebrecht) president of Odebrecht, was arrested on charges that he had paid politicians \$230 million in bribes[.\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-mo-154) Three other company executives were also arrested, as well as the chief executive of [Andrade Gutierrez,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrade_Gutierrez) another construction conglomerate.[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-155)

## ![](_page_13_Picture_10.jpeg)

## Protests related to 2016 Lula's testimony

On 4 March 2016, as part of "Operation Car Wash", Brazilian authorities raided Lula's home[.\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-Romero-156)[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-157) After the raid, the police detained Lula for questioning.[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-euronews-158)[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-Machado-159) A police statement alleged that Lula had collaborated in illegal bribes from the oil company [Petrobras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrobras) to benefit his political party and presidential campaign.[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-euronews-158) Prosecutor Carlos Fernando said, "The favors to Lula from big construction companies involved in the fraud at Petrobras were many and hard to quantify".[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-citizen-160) Lula said that he and his party were being politically persecuted[.\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-forbes-un-161)[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-folha-lavajato-162)[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-infomoney-lavajato-163)

On 16 March 2016, Rousseff appointed Lula as her [chief of sta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Staff_of_Brazil)ff, a position comparable to that of prime minister. This would have shielded him from arrest due to the immunity that went with the position[.\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-164) Cabinet ministers in Brazil are among close to seven hundred senior government officials enjoying special judicial standing, which means they can only be tried by Brazil's [Supreme Federal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Federal_Court) [Court.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Federal_Court) Supreme Court Judge [Gilmar Mendes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilmar_Mendes) suspended Lula's appointment on the grounds that Rousseff was trying to help Lula circumvent prosecution.[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-165)[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-166)[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-bbcjudge-167)[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-168)

On 28 July 2016, Lula filed a petition with the [United Nations Human Rights Committee,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Committee) a panel of experts, requesting that it provide a view on his accusation that Moro had violated his rights.[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-169) The Committee ultimately accepted the case[.\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-170)

On 14 September 2016, prosecutors filed corruption charges against Lula, accusing him of being the mastermind or 'maximum commander of the scheme'[.\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-171) On 19 September 2016, 13th Circuit [\(Paraná\)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paran%C3%A1_(state)) federal judge [Sergio Moro,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Moro) who was leading the corruption probe, accepted an indictment for money laundering against Lula and his wife [Marisa Letícia Lula da Silva.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marisa_Let%C3%ADcia_Lula_da_Silva) On 11 May 2017, Lula answered a summons by appearing in [Curitiba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curitiba) and was questioned by Moro. The closed-court hearing lasted five hours. Thousands of Lula supporters went to Curitiba, together with Dilma Rousseff. After the hearing, Lula and Rousseff gave speeches to his supporters; Lula attacked what he called bias in the Brazilian media[.\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-172)

## ![](_page_14_Picture_5.jpeg)

Demonstrators gather in front of the [Palácio do Planalto,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pal%C3%A1cio_do_Planalto) the presidential palace, to protest against Lula's appointment as Chief of Staff [of the Presidency,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Staff_of_Brazil) 16 March 2016.

#### Guilty verdict and sentencing

Lula was found guilty by the lower court of accepting R\$3.7 million in bribes (\$940,000 US) in the form of improvements to his beachfront house, made by construction company [Grupo Metha,](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Grupo_Metha&action=edit&redlink=1) which in turn received lucrative contracts from the state-owned oil company [Petrobras.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrobras)[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-nyt-prison-173) Lula also faced other charges, including money laundering, influence peddling and obstruction of justice[.\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-174)[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-nyt-prison-173) On 12 July 2017, [Sergio Moro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Moro) sentenced Lula to nine and a half years in prison[.\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-175) Lula remained free pending his appeal.[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-sentence-176) Lula's lawyer accused the judge of bias and the judge replied that nobody, not even the former president, should be above the [rule of law.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rule_of_law)[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-sentence-176)

On 25 January 2018, the Appeal Court of Porto Alegre found Lula guilty of corruption and money laundering and increased his sentence to 12 years of priso[n\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-177) for one of the nine charges, while the other eight were still pending. On 26 March 2018, that same court upheld its own sentence, thus ending the case in that court[.\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-178)

## ![](_page_14_Picture_10.jpeg)

Lula is sworn in as Chief of Staff by President [Dilma Rousse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dilma_Rousseff)ff on 17 March 2016.

### Prison

On 5 April 2018, Brazil's Supreme Federal Court (STF) voted 6–5 to deny Lula's [habeas corpus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habeas_corpus) petition.[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-179) The court ruled that Lula must begin serving his sentence relating to his 12 July 2017 graft conviction for taking bribes from an engineering firm in return for help land contracts with state-run oil company [Petroleo Brasileiro SA,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petroleo_Brasileiro_SA) despite his not having exhausted all of his appeals, but rather only one appeal, at which the appellate court had unanimously upheld his sentence[.\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-180)[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-181)[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto1-182) Brazilian financial markets rallied, as the decision increased the chances a marketfriendly candidate winning the election.[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto1-182) Lula and his party vowed to continue his campaign from prison following the court's decision that he must surrender himself by 6 April[.\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-183) The head of Brazil's army, General Eduardo Villas Boas, called for Lula to be placed behind bars[.\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-184) Following Judge Moro's issuance of an arrest warrant for Lula on 6 April 2018, Lula appealed to the UN Human Rights Committee to - in addition to his primary case - take emergency action by asking the government of Brazil to prevent his arrest until he had exhausted all appeals, arguing that the Brazilian Supreme Court had narrowly adopted its ruling with only six votes against five, which "shows the need for an independent court"; the Committee ultimately denied Lula's request.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-185)[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-186) In June 2018, the Committee denied Lula's request[.\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-187)

Lula failed to turn himself in at the scheduled time,[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-188) but he did so on the following day on 7 April 2018[.\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-189) After Lula was imprisoned, protesters took to the streets in cities across Brazil[.\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-190) Lula's imprisonment led to the formation of the [Free Lula Movement.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Lula_Movement)

On 28 May 2018, the UN Human Rights Committee initiated an investigation into Lula's case.[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-191) In August, the Committee "requested Brazil to take all necessary measures to ensure that Lula can enjoy and exercise his political rights while in prison, as candidate in the 2018 presidential elections"; Brazil's foreign ministry noted that the recommendation had no legal significance[.\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-192)[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-193)

On 8 July 2018, [on-call](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/On-call) weekend duty federal judge for the 4th region Rogério Favreto ordered Lula's release.[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-194) Moro said that Favreto did not have the authority to release Lula, and Favreto's ruling was overturned the same day by the Judge Pedro Gebran Neto, president of the 4th regional court[.\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-195)

On 2 August 2018, Pope Francis received three former allies of Lula in Rome: [Celso Amorim,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celso_Amorim) [Alberto Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Fern%C3%A1ndez) and [Carlos Ominami.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Ominami)[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-196) Later, the pope addressed a handwritten note to Lula (posted on his Twitter account) with the following text: "To Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva with my blessing, asking him to pray for me, Francisco".[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-197)

On 9 June 2019, [The Intercept](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Intercept) published [leaked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaza_Jato) [Telegram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telegram_(app)) messages between the judge in Lula's case, [Sergio Moro,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergio_Moro) and the Operation Car Wash lead prosecutor, [Deltan Dallagnol,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deltan_Dallagnol) in which they allegedly conspired to convict Lula to prevent his candidacy for the 2018 presidential election.[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-198) [\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-199)[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-200)[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-201)[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-202)[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-203)[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-204) Moro was accused of lacking impartiality in Lula's trial[.\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-nytimes-205) Following the disclosures, the resumption of legal proceedings was determined by the Supreme Court.[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-206) Moro denied any wrongdoing or [judicial misconduct](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_misconduct) during the course of Operation Car Wash and his investigation of Lula, claiming that the conversations leaked by The Intercept were misrepresented by the press and that conversations between prosecutors and judges are normal[.\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-207) Moro became [Minister of Justice and Public Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Justice_and_Public_Security_(Brazil)) after the election of president [Jair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_Bolsonaro) [Bolsonaro.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_Bolsonaro)

The information published by The Intercept prompted reactions both in Brazil and overseas. A group of 17 lawyers, ministers of Justice, and high court members from eight countries reacted to the leaks by describing formera Lula as a political prisoner and calling for his release[.\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-choc-208) United

States Senator [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders) said Lula should be released and his conviction annulled. American Congressman [Ro Khanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ro_Khanna) asked the Trump administration to investigate Lula's case, saying that "Moro was a bad actor and part of a larger conspiracy to send Lula to jail".[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-209) A number of international intellectuals, activists and political leaders, including professor [Noam Chomsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noam_Chomsky) and 12 US Congressmen, complained that the legal proceedings appeared to be designed to prevent Lula from running for president in 2018.[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-210)[\[211\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-211) American talk show host [Michael Brooks,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Brooks_(political_commentator)) a vocal advocate for Lula, opined that Lula's imprisonment and Moro's alleged political motives had rendered the results of the 2018 election "fundamentally illegitimate"[.\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-212)

#### Release

On 8 November 2019, Lula was released from prison after 580 days when the Brazilian Supreme Court ended mandatory imprisonment of convicted criminals after their first appeal failed[.\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-213)[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-:0-214)[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-215)[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-216)[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-217)[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-218) His release allowed him to remain out of prison until all of his appeals of his corruption and money laundering convictions were exhausted[.\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-219)

On 27 November, the [Federal Regional Tribunal of Region 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Federal_Regional_Tribunal_of_Region_4&action=edit&redlink=1) in [Porto Alegre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porto_Alegre) increased Lula's sentence to 17 years.[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-220)

On 8 March 2021, Judge [Edson Fachin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edson_Fachin) of the Supreme Federal Court annulled all convictions against Lula, ruling that the court in Curitiba, in [Paraná](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paran%C3%A1_(state)) state, which convicted him lacked jurisdiction to do so because the crimes he was accused of did not take place in that state, as at the time Lula resided in the capital, Brasilia[.\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto4-221) Justice Fachin said the cases against him should therefore be retried by a court in that city[.\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-222) The judge did not rule as to whether Lula was guilty or not of the corruption charges[.\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto4-221) On 15 April, a full Supreme Court upheld the ruling in an 8–3 decision[.\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-223)

On 23 March 2021, the Supreme Federal Court ruled by a 3–2 decision that Moro, who had overseen Lula's trial in a case, was biased against him. [\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-biased-224) On 23 June it upheld the ruling in a 7–4 decision[.\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-225) On 24 June, Judge Gilmar Mendes of the Supreme Federal Court annulled the two other cases Moro had brought against Lula, reasoning that there was a link between them and the case in which Moro was declared biased. This meant that all evidence Moro had collected against Lula was inadmissible in court, and that fresh trials would be needed.[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-Gilmar-226)

# First post-presidency

## Health

On 29 October 2011, it was announced that Lula, a former smoker, had a [malignant tumor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malignant_tumor) in his [larynx.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larynx)[\[227\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-227) His tumor was detected after his voice became unusually hoarse[.\[228\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-228) He had [chemotherapy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemotherapy) to counteract the tumor[.\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-229) He was later treated with [radiation therapy,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radiation_therapy) and his [laryngeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laryngeal_cancer) [cancer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laryngeal_cancer) went into remission. Lula announced his recovery in March 2012[.\[230\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-230)

On 21 January 2021, Lula tested positive for [COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Cuba) while participating in the filming of an [Oliver Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oliver_Stone) documentary in [Cuba,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuba) five days after arriving on the island. He did not need to be admitted to a hospital, and was able to recover[.\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-231) On 13 March 2021, Lula received his first dose of the [Corona Vac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corona Vac) vaccine.[\[232\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-232)

In October 2023 Lula had [hip joint replacement surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip_replacement) to implant a [hip prosthesis,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Implant_(medicine)) replacing the top of his right [femur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Femur) with an implant to treat his [arthrosis.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthrosis)[\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto34-233)[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto31-234) [\[235\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-235) He also had a [blepharoplasty,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blepharoplasty) a cosmetic plastic surgery to remove excess skin from both of his eyelids[.\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto34-233)[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-236)[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto31-234)

### 2018 presidential campaign

In 2017, Lula announced he would stand as the Workers' Party candidate for president again in the [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Brazilian_general_election) [election.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Brazilian_general_election) In September, he led a caravan of supporters which travelled through the states of Brazil, starting with Minas Gerais, whose governor was Lula's political ally [Fernando Pimentel.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Pimentel)[\[237\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-237) While traveling through the [South of Brazil,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Region,_Brazil) the caravan became the target of protests. In Paraná, a campaign bus was shot, and in Rio Grande do Sul, rocks were thrown at pro-Lula militants.[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-238)

Despite Lula's imprisonment in April 2018, the Workers' Party kept Lula as the party's presidential candidate. In a poll conducted by [Ibope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Institute_of_Public_Opinion_and_Statistics) in June 2018, Lula led with 33% of vote intentions, with the [PSL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Liberal_Party_(Brazil)) candidate [Jair Bolsonaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_Bolsonaro) polling second with 15%[.\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-239) Lula negotiated a national coalition with the [PCdoB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partido_Comunista_do_Brasil) and regional alliances with the [Socialist Party.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partido_Socialista_Brasileiro)[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-240)

The Workers' Party officially nominated Lula as its candidate on 5 August 2018, in São Paulo. Actor [Sérgio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A9rgio_Mamberti) [Mamberti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A9rgio_Mamberti) read a letter written by Lula, who was unable to attend because of his prison sentence. Former São Paulo mayor Fernando Haddad was named as Lula's running mate and intended to represent Lula in events and debates. In the event that Lula were declared ineligible, Haddad would replace Lula as candidate, with [Manuela d'Ávila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuela_d%27%C3%81vila) replacing Haddad as the vice presidential candidate.[\[241\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-241)

In response to a petition from Lula, the [UN Human Rights Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Committee) on 17 August 2018 suggested to the Brazilian government that it allow Lula to exercise his political rights.[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-242)

In a 26 August poll, Lula had 39 percent of vote intentions within one month of the first round. The same opinion polling put Lula ahead of all his challengers in a second round run-off, including the nearest one, [PSL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Liberal_Party_(Brazil)) candidate Jair Bolsonaro, by 52 to 32.[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-243)

## ![](_page_17_Picture_10.jpeg)

## Lula, 2016

## ![](_page_17_Picture_12.jpeg)

Lula with Brazilian politicians [Manuela d'Ávila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuela_d%27%C3%81vila) and [Marcelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo_Freixo) [Freixo,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo_Freixo) 2018

Lula's candidacy was denied by the [Superior Electoral Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Electoral_Court) on 31 August 2018, as the majority of the seven-judge panel voted to bar Lula from running in the presidential race.[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-244) On 11 September 2018, Lula officially dropped out of the election and was replaced by Fernando Haddad, whom Lula endorsed[.\[245\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-245)

# Second presidency (2023–present)

## 2022 election

In May 2021, Lula said that he would run for a third term in the October 2022 general election, against the incumbent President [Jair Bolsonaro.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_Bolsonaro)[\[246\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-Valor2021-05-20a-246)[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-Am Quarterly2021-07-07a-247)[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-Guardian2021-07-30a-248) He was 17% ahead of Bolsonaro in a poll in January 2022[.\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-Nasdaq2022-01-14a-249) In April 2022, Lula announced that his running mate would be [Geraldo Alckmin,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geraldo_Alckmin) a three-term governor of São Paulo state who had run against Lula in the 2006 presidential elections.[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-250)

On 2 October, in the first round vote, Lula was in first place with 48% of the electorate, qualifying for the second round with Bolsonaro, who received 43% of the votes. Lula was elected in the second round on 30 October with 50.89% of the vote, the smallest margin in the history of Brazil's presidential elections[,\[251\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-251) [\[252\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-252) three days after his 77th birthday. He became the first president of Brazil elected to three terms, the first since [Getúlio Vargas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Get%C3%BAlio_Vargas) to serve in non-consecutive terms, and the first to unseat an incumbent president. He was sworn in on 1 January 2023.[\[253\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-253)[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-254) At age 77, he was the oldest Brazilian president at the time of inauguration.[\[255\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-255)[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-256)[\[257\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-Boadle-257)

## ![](_page_18_Picture_7.jpeg)

Lula with [President of Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Argentina) [Alberto Fernández,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Fern%C3%A1ndez) 2022

### Tenure

Lula said that his main commitments were: the reconstruction of the country in the face of the economic crisis; democracy, sovereignty and peace; economic development and stability; fighting poverty; education; implementation of a National System of Culture and the expansion of housing programs[.\[258\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-258) He has held the presidency of the [G20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G20) since 2023[.\[259\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-259)

Lula's popularity declined; in September 2023 38% of those polled considered him to be good or excellent, while 30% considered him to be average, and 31% viewed him as bad or terrible.[\[3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto6-3)

### Foreign policy

### China

In March 2023, Lula met in China with Chinese President [Xi Jinping,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi_Jinping) and signed 15 memoranda of understanding and 20 agreements on a wide range of issues[.\[260\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-260) Lula gave a speech in which he said that no one would keep Brazil from improving its relationship with China—which was taken as a reference to the United States[.\[261\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-261)[\[262\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-262) In January 2024, Lula reaffirmed to Chinese Foreign Minister [Wang Yi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Yi_(politician)) his recognition of China's policy of "one China", under which China claims Taiwan[.\[263\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-263) [Human Rights Watch,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch) in the meantime, cautioned Lula against cozying up to China, while remaining silent about [China's human rights abuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_China)[.\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto11-101)[\[264\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-264)[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto13-265)

#### Cuba

In September 2023, Lula called Cuba a "victim" of an "illegal" [United States embargo against Cuba.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_embargo_against_Cuba)[\[266\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto15-266) [\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto13-265) He also denounced the inclusion of Cuba on the [US list of state sponsors of terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Sponsors_of_Terrorism_(U. S._list))[.\[266\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto15-266)[\[267\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-267)

#### Iran

In March 2023, Lula's administration allowed two [Iranian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Navy) warships, [forward base ship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expeditionary_Mobile_Base) [IRIS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRIS_Makran) [Makran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRIS_Makran) and [frigate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moudge-class_frigate) [IRIS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRIS_Dena) [Dena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRIS_Dena), to dock in Rio de Janeiro.[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-268)[\[269\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-269)[\[270\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto14-270) U. S. Ambassador to Brazil [Elizabeth Bagley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Bagley) said that in the past the warships had facilitated terrorist activities, and US Senator [Ted Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Cruz) said that "the docking of Iranian warships in Brazil is a dangerous development and a direct threat to the safety and security of Americans."[\[270\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto14-270)

## ![](_page_19_Picture_7.jpeg)

Lula and Chinese President [Xi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi_Jinping) [Jinping,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi_Jinping) April 2023

Lula endorsed admitting Iran into the [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS) club, and in August 2023 met with met with Iranian president [Ebrahim Raeisi.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebrahim_Raeisi)[\[271\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-271)[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto12-272) Lula stopped short of condemning [Iran's rights abuses.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_the_Islamic_Republic_of_Iran)[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto12-272)

#### Nicaragua

In June 2023, at an [Organization of American States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_of_American_States) (OAS) summit, Lula tried to soften OAS criticism of Nicaragua's government, which was accused of repression and of violations of human rights and property rights.[\[273\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-273)[\[274\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-274)[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-275)[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto16-276) Nicaragua's former ambassador to the OAS, [Arturo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arturo_Mc Fields) [Mc Fields,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arturo_Mc Fields) said that the proposed softening was "shameful," and that "President Lula is lying and telling another story that never existed in Nicaragua."[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto16-276)

### Russia

In May 2023, he declined an invitation to the [Saint Petersburg International Economic Forum,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Petersburg_International_Economic_Forum) saying that he "can't visit Russia at the moment", while confirming that he had spoken to Putin.[\[277\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-277) In December 2023, Lula said that he will invite Vladimir Putin to Brazil[.\[278\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-278) In February 2024, he was visited by Russian Foreign Minister [Sergey Lavrov.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergey_Lavrov)[\[279\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-279)

#### Russian invasion of Ukraine

Lula has commented often on the [Russian invasion of Ukraine.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) Ukraine described his comments as "Russian attempts to distort the truth".[\[280\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-Reuters_ukraine_response-280)[\[281\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-281)

In May 2022, Lula placed blame on Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy) for Russia's invasion of Ukraine, saying "This guy is as responsible as Putin for the war".[\[282\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-282) Lula also repeatedly attacked [NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO) and the [European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union) as having caused the war. He accused NATO of "claiming for itself the right to install military bases in the vicinity of another country".[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-283) In April 2023, Lula declared after a state visit to China that "the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States) needs to stop encouraging war and start talking about peace". U. S. [National](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Council) [Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Council) spokesman [John Kirby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kirby_(admiral)) responded by accusing Lula of "parroting Russian and Chinese propaganda", describing his comments as "simply misguided" and "suggesting the United States and Europe are somehow not interested in peace, or that we share responsibility for the war".[\[284\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-284)

## ![](_page_20_Picture_5.jpeg)

Lula and Nicaraguan Foreign Minister Denis Moncada Colindres, January 2023

In April 2023, Lula initially condemned Russia's violation of Ukraine's territorial integrity and said Russia should withdraw from Ukrainian territory it has occupied since February 2022.[\[285\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-reu19-285) Later, however, that same month, he suggested that Ukraine should "give up [Crimea"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimea) to Russia in exchange for [peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) and Russia's withdrawal from Ukrainian territory it [occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_of_Ukraine) after February 2022, saying Zelenskyy "cannot want everything".[\[286\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-286)[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-287)

After Germany appealed to Lula to [provide military aid to Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) by selling it arms, Lula refused[.\[288\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-288) On 26 April, in a joint press conference, Spanish prime minister [Pedro Sánchez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_S%C3%A1nchez) questioned Lula's position, stressing that as the victimized country Ukraine needed to be supported[.\[289\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-elmu-289) Lula said that he is seeking peace in accordance with a binding foreign policy principle in the pacifist [Brazilian Constitution of 1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Constitution_of_1988)[.\[290\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-290) He said that the countries of the [Global South,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_North_and_Global_South) including Brazil, India, [Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia) and [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China) "want peace", but both Putin and Zelenskyy "are convinced that they are going to win the war" and do not want to talk about peace.[\[291\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-291) He noted the [human cost of the war,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) as well as the war's impact on [food security,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%932023_food_crises) [energy costs,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%932023_global_energy_crisis) and [global supply chains.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_supply_chain)[\[292\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-292)[\[293\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-293)

### Saudi Arabia

In November 2023, Lula met in [Riyadh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riyadh) with the prime minister and crown prince of [Saudi Arabia,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia) [Mohammed bin Salman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_bin_Salman)[.\[294\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-294)[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto18-295)[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto17-296) They discussed strengthening bilateral relations, and investments in both countries.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto18-295)[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto17-296) Salman said that a more robust strategic partnership between the two countries would benefit both sides.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto18-295) The \$10 billion that the [sovereign wealth fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sovereign_wealth_fund) of Saudi Arabia pledged to invest in Brazil was one topic of conversation.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto18-295)[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto17-296) Lula mentioned Brazil's rapprochement with Arab countries[.\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto18-295) Salman also discussed Saudi Arabia's entry into [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS) in January 2024.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto18-295) Lula invited Salman to visit Brazil in 2024[.\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto18-295)[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-297)[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto17-296)

In February 2024, Saudi Ambassador to Brazil Faisal Ghulam participated in a reception held by the ambassadors of [Arab countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_countries) and [Islamic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_countries)

[countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_countries) in honor of Lula, and on behalf of the ambassadors of the Arab and Islamic countries, Ghulam delivered a speech in which he reviewed the developing relations between the Arab and Islamic countries and Brazil.[\[298\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-298)

#### Israel–Hamas war

Lula condemned the [Hamas attack on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war) carried out on 7 October 2023[.\[299\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-299) On 11 October 2023, he called for a ceasefire in the [Israel–Hamas war,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war) stating, it was "urgently needed in defense of Israeli and Palestinian children".[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-300) Lula urged [Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas) to release [kidnapped](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidnappings_during_the_2023_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war) Israeli children and Israel to stop bombing the [Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip) and allow Palestinian children and their mothers to leave the war zone.[\[301\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-301) On 25 October 2023, Lula stated, "It's not a war, it's a [genocide"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation)[.\[302\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-302) On 18 February 2024, he drew comparisons to the [Nazi Holocaust.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust)[\[303\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-303) That same day, he was [accused of "blatant antisemitism"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weaponization_of_antisemitism) by [Dani Dayan,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dani_Dayan) the Chairman of [Yad Vashem,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yad_Vashem) the World Holocaust Remembrance Center, for comparing Israel's actions to Adolf Hitler[.\[304\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-304) Brazil's ambassador to Israel [Frederico Meyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frederico_Meyer) was recalled after these comments, and

## ![](_page_21_Picture_5.jpeg)

Lula and Saudi Head of State in [Saudi Arabia,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia) 2023.

President Lula was designated a [persona non grata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persona_non_grata) in the State of Israel.[\[305\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-305)[\[306\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-306) Lula subsequently declined to apologise and despite having invoked a comparison with Adolf Hitler, he stated "I did not say the word Holocaust, that was the interpretation of the prime minister of Israel, it was not mine.["\[307\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-307)

#### United States

In April 2023, alluding to the support of the United States for Ukraine in the wake of the Russian invasion of Ukraine, Lula said that the US needs to "stop encouraging war.["\[308\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-308) In May 2023, he said that US economic sanctions on Venezuela were "worse than a war" and "kill" women and children.[\[309\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-309) In February 2024, [University of Sao Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Sao_Paulo) foreign policy expert Feliciano de Sa Guimaraes said Lula only listens to one side in his government, "the left-wing, anti-American voices who very aggressively speak of a radical change in the global order."[\[310\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-310)

### Venezuela

Lula restored diplomatic ties that Brazil had cut off with Venezuela's [Nicolas Maduro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas_Maduro) government after 2018 elections that had been [condemned by critics as a sham.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_presidential_crisis) In March 2023, Lula refused to join 54 other nations and sign a United Nations declaration criticizing Venezuala's human rights abuses[.\[311\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-311) In May 2023 Lula met with Maduro in Brazil[.\[312\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-312)

## ![](_page_21_Picture_12.jpeg)

Lula and US President [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden) at the [White House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House) on 10 February 2023

In May 2023, Lula warmly embraced and fully supported Venezuela's authoritarian leftist President [Nicolás](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro) [Maduro.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro)[\[313\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto7-313) Lula dismissed charges against Maduro of [human rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights) and [civil rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_rights) abuses as a political "constructed narrative.["\[128\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto8-128) Lula was criticized by Uruguay's President [Luis Lacalle Pou,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Lacalle_Pou) who said that the "worst thing we can do" is pretend there are no significant human rights problems in Venezuela, and by Chile's President [Gabriel Boric,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Boric) who said that Lula was making light of [human rights violations in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Venezuela) [Venezuela.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Venezuela)[\[313\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto7-313) Lula also criticized as "unjustified" [U. S. sanctions on Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctions_during_the_Venezuelan_crisis) for its alleged human rights abuses, and criticized the United States for denying the legitimacy of Maduro, who the U. S. said had not allowed free elections[.\[314\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-314)[\[315\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-315)[\[316\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-316)

## ![](_page_22_Picture_3.jpeg)

Lula meeting with Venezuelan President [Nicolás Maduro,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro) May 2023

#### Economy

## ![](_page_22_Picture_6.jpeg)

Lula and other BRICS leaders at the [15th BRICS Summit,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/15th_BRICS_summit) August 2023, in Johannesburg.

In March 2023 Lula reinforced the Bolsa Família program. The program was created during the first term of Lula and then significantly cut by [Jair Bolsonaro.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_Bolsonaro) The program should help around 60 million Brazilians suffering from poverty[.\[317\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-317) According to [the World Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_World_Bank) estimates, the reinforced program will reduce the poverty rate in Brazil to 24.3% - the level before the [COVID-19 pandemic.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic)[\[318\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-318)[\[319\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-319)

In August 2023, Lula announced a vast infrastructure investment program of over \$350 billion over four years. Part of this sum is earmarked to finance the "My home, my life" social housing project. The program also includes 100 billion for energy and 65 billion for transport and roads. Education and health are also concerned, with the construction of schools and hospitals. The project also aims to boost economic growth and develop [clean energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clean_energy)[.\[320\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-320)[\[321\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-321)

At the beginning of September, he presented a major plan to eradicate hunger, as 33 million Brazilians do not have enough to eat, and more than half the country is affected to varying degrees by some form of [food insecurity.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_security) To this end, he set up a national network of food banks to prevent wastage, increased the budget allocated to school meals and increased the purchase of food from family farms to supply public canteens. These measures are part of a broader policy to build social housing and raise the minimum wage and other social benefits. The fight against world hunger is also high on the Brazilian president's international agenda.[\[322\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-322)[\[323\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-323)

In the first quarter of 2023 Brazilian economy grew by 1.9%. In the second quarter by 0.9%, 3 times more than expected, while many of the neighbors of Brazil saw a shrinkage in their economy. The possible reasons of this phenomenon include reduced inflation, a good harvest, and an improved credit rating. The economic policy of Lula regarding taxation, spendings, public ownership of some companies probably played a major role in this. Explaining his economic philosophy Lula once said: ""[Brazilians] need to understand that the money that exists in this country needs to circulate in the hands of many people," "We do not want the concentration of wealth. We want more people to have access to credit to make the wheel of the economy turn. The growing economy needs to be distributed.["\[324\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-324)

#### Environment

## ![](_page_23_Picture_4.jpeg)

Launch of the [Global Biofuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Biofuel_Alliance) [Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Biofuel_Alliance) at the [2023 G20 New](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_G20_New_Delhi_summit) [Delhi summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_G20_New_Delhi_summit)

During his campaign Lula pledged to end [illegal logging.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegal_logging)[\[325\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-325) In 2004, Lula had presented a road map for curbing [deforestation.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation) It was part of "The Action Plan for the Prevention and Control of Deforestation in the Legal Amazon", which sought to decrease deforestation in the Amazon by 80% by 2020.[\[326\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-326) This plan was largely responsible for the 83% decrease in the Amazon deforestation rate in the years 2004 through 2012, but it was suspended during Bolsonaro's presidency. Lula re-affirmed the plan's goals in his third term, with a new target of zero illegal deforestation by the year 2030.[\[327\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-327) The plan includes different measures for creating a sustainable economy in the Amazon region, like [bioeconomy,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bioeconomy) rural credits and managed fishing. [\[328\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-328)

According to Amazon Conservation's MAAP forest monitoring program, the deforestation rate in the Brazilian Amazon from the 1

of January to the 8 of November 2023 decreased by 59% in comparison to the same period in 2022[.\[329\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-329) [\[330\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-330)[\[331\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-331) In July 2023 the deforestation rate was 66% lower than in July 2022. In the beginning of August Lula participated in the Belem summit, 8 Amazonian nations renewed [the Amazon cooperation treaty.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_Cooperation_Treaty_Organization)[\[332\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-332) [\[333\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-333)

However, there are concerns that illegal loggers have partly moved their action from the Amazon rainforest to [Cerrado,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cerrado) where the environmental destruction has increased.[\[334\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-334)

## ![](_page_23_Picture_10.jpeg)

The [Amazon rainforest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_rainforest) near [Manaus,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manaus) Brazil

As a whole, the rate of primary forest loss declined in Brazil by 36% in 2023.[\[335\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-335)

Bolsonaro had strongly cut spending for security in the Brazilian Amazon, and in 2022 34 environmental defenders were murdered in this region. When Lula re-assumed office, he sent troops to restore law enforcement in the region. As of October 2023 there were still "reports of violence, threats, torture, intimidation, attempts at criminalization and other non-lethal violations".[\[336\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-336)

In April Biden pledged to give \$500 million to the [Amazon Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Amazon_Fund) which was frozen during the rule of Bolsonaro and reactivated when Lula returned to power, to deal with [climate change"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change).[\[337\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-337) According to [John Kerry,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) the overall financial help from US to Brazil for stopping deforestation through different channels will be around 2 billion US dollars[.\[338\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-338)

Lula and the French president [Emmanuel Macron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmanuel_Macron) agreed about cooperation between Brazil and France on different environmental issues, including the transmission of 1.1 billion dollars for preserving the Amazon rainforest[.\[339\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-339)

Lula pledged to recognize 14 new [indigenous reserves.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_territory_(Brazil)) Six were recognized as of May 2023[.\[340\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-340) Lula and American president Joe Biden committed to work together on the issue[.\[341\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-341)

Several hours after Lula talked about leaving fossil fuels at [COP 28,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference) his government held an auction in which it offered 603 territorial blocks for oil extraction. The territories cover 2% of the territory of Brazil, overlap with many protected areas or areas belonging to indigenous people and can result in a release of 1 gigaton of CO2[.\[342\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-342)

#### Freedom of the press

In March 2023, the Lula government launched a campaign to fight "misinformation".[\[343\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto25-343)[\[344\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-344) The initiative was viewed by many as a tool for Lula's administration to delegitimize criticism it faces--under the guise of "fact-checking", and raised serious concerns about freedom of expression[.\[345\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-345)[\[343\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto25-343) In response, the senior programme director of the [International Center for Journalists,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Center_for_Journalists) Christina Tardáliga, tweeted "There is no such thing as government fact-checking. This appropriation of the term is misguided and offensive. What the government does is propaganda."[\[343\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto25-343)

# Political positions and philosophy

In Brazil, liberal is often avoided by [leftists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leftists) because of connotations with pro-business policies. He advocated ["socialism of the 21st century"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism_of_the_21st_century), but Lulism is considered to be substantially similar to [social liberalism.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_liberalism)[\[346\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-346)[\[347\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-Social Liberal-347)[\[348\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-348) Although he showed a moderate centre-left liberal tendency economically, he highlighted his closeness with the [Bolivarian Republic of Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivarian_Republic_of_Venezuela) and negatively evaluated [Juan Guaidó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Guaid%C3%B3) during the [Venezuelan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_crisis) [crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_crisis)[.\[349\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-349) He is "personally against" abortion, but maintains that it should be treated as a [public health issue.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unsafe_abortion#Health_risks)[\[350\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-350)[\[351\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-351)

#### Palestine

Lula criticized the decisions by [Western countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_countries) to cut funding to UNRWA and in response pledged to the [Palestinian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Palestine) that Brazil would increase its funding to UNRWA. Lula has called for a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution) with Palestine "definitively recognised as a full and sovereign state". [\[352\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto-352)

On 18 February 2024, Lula told reporters in Addis Ababa while attending the African Union Summit, "What's happening in the [Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip) isn't a war, it's a [genocide.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide) ... It's not a war of soldiers against soldiers. It's a war between a highly prepared army and women and children. ... What's happening in the Gaza Strip with the [Palestinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian) people hasn't happened at any other moment in history. Actually, it has happened: when [Hitler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf_Hitler) decided to kill the [Jews"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews)[.\[352\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-auto-352)

# In popular culture

Academy Award-nominated film Brazilian director [Fábio Barreto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A1bio_Barreto) directed the 2009 Brazilian biographical film [Lula, Son of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lula,_Son_of_Brazil) that depicts the life of Lula up to 35 years of age.[\[393\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-393) Upon its release, it was the most expensive Brazilian film ever.[\[394\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-394) The film was a commercial and critical failure[.\[395\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-395)[\[396\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-396) Critics charged that it was election propaganda, fostering a [cult of personality.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cult_of_personality)[\[397\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-397)[\[398\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-398)[\[399\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-399)

The Netflix Brazilian series [The Mechanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Mechanism_(TV_series)) deals with Operation Car Wash, and has a charachter inspired by Lula, who is referred to as João Higino in the series[.\[400\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-400)[\[401\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-401)

The 2019 Brazilian documentary [The Edge of Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Edge_of_Democracy), written and directed by [Petra Costa,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petra_Costa) chronicled the rise and fall of Lula and Dilma Rousseff, and the socio-political upheaval in Brazil during the period.[\[402\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luiz_In%C3%A1cio_Lula_da_Silva#cite_note-402)
